# Backlog triage - 2026-09-15

Every entry in [`docs/BACKLOG.md`](../BACKLOG.md) as of 2026-09-15, given a verdict by a read-only
investigation of the codebase. **Read this before triaging the backlog again** - the point of
writing it down is that nobody has to repeat the exercise. Re-triage only the entries added or
changed since this date.

This complements, and does not replace, the document map in
[outstanding-work-index-2026-09-14.md](./outstanding-work-index-2026-09-14.md): that one maps
*which documents* hold open work; this one gives *each core backlog entry* a verdict.

**How the verdicts were reached.** 284 entries in 36 batches, each investigated against the
code, not the prose. Any entry claimed `done` or `stale` was then given to **two independent
verifiers told to refute it**, and closed only if both confirmed. Anything refuted is kept open
as `needs-review`. The machine-readable version, keyed by entry title so it survives line
shifts, is [triage-2026-09-15.json](./triage-2026-09-15.json).

| verdict | count |
|---|---|
| Verified DONE - removed from BACKLOG.md | 1 |
| Claimed done/stale but REFUTED - kept open | 14 |
| Actionable by an agent now | 137 |
| Blocked on another entry | 11 |
| Needs live infrastructure, a live provider, or hardware to prove | 46 |
| Needs the operator | 75 |
| **total** | **284** |

  - The connector list now sends a credential-free `expired` boolean on every connection, and Identity Hub reads it for the Reconnect pill, the '· expired' marker, the Need-attention tile count and the needs-attention filter. - **The ticket-row escalation mirror outlives the escalation it describes (2026-09-15)**  `S · no-docker · core` — **CLOSED 2026-09-19**
  - Re-derived: closed by `60ca36ff` ("the ticket row's transition mirror describes the transition that just happened").
    `buildTicketRowStatusMetadataPatch` no longer returns null — it always emits `lastStatusTransition: {status, ...metadata}`,
    so a de-escalation with no metadata replaces the mirror instead of leaving the old escalation in it. The guard the entry
    asked for exists: `tests/unit/ticket-status-history-metadata.spec.ts:176` "replaces the escalation mirror when a ticket
    leaves escalated carrying no metadata". The entry's optional half — clearing the stale top-level reason/statusSource/
    nextAction — is moot: `deriveTicketEscalationDetail` reads only the nested mirror, `buildDetail` returns null without a
    reason, and `statusSource` has ZERO readers anywhere in src/.  _(Animatronics and the maker labs)_
  - The defect is still in the tree. When a ticket leaves 'escalated' with no metadata, the ticket-row mirror is never touched: the patch builder returns null for empty metadata, and buildStatusTransitionMetadata hands back the empty base for any target except escalated/dead_letter. Both stores then take their no-patch arm, and deriveTicketEscalationDetail still falls back to the mirror. No guard covers the de-escalate-with-no-metadata shape. The fix is small: always emit lastStatusTransition:{status} (and optionally clear the stale top-level reason/statusSource/nextAction) when the target is not escalated, plus a unit guard through the in-memory store that asserts on the mirror directly. The patch keys don't overlap metadata.source, so queue/app provenance is not overwritten.
  - evidence: src/entities/ticket/ticket-status-row-metadata.ts:28 `return Object.keys(metadata).length > 0 ? patch : null;`; src/features/ticketing/services/ticket-service.ts:500 `if (toStatus !== 'escalated') { return base; }`; no-patch arms at src/features/ticketing/services/ticket-store-postgres.ts:299 and src/features/ticketing/services/in-memory-ticket-store.ts:162; mirror fallback at src/entities/ticket/ticket-escalation-detail.ts:53 and :74. Entry filed by 09503c5d (2026-09-14), which fixed only the recency path. Existing specs tests/unit/ticket-escalation-detail.spec.ts and ticket-status-history-me

Fix: escape the quotes or pass the metadata via stdin, a file or discrete flags; move the session guard ahead of Enter-SharedNodeLease; add a powershell.exe round-trip spec. The done-when's scheduled end-to-end run then needs the live stack and the render node.

- **Ambient Recall — relevance floor on "possibly related" hits**  `S · docker · core`  _(Application-package follow-ups)_
  - There is no similarity floor. relatedRecall keeps every hybrid-RRF hit it gets back, filtered only by segment, owner, profile and day. The RRF score is rank-based, and the vector leg's cosine similarity is thrown away during fusion. The fix is a floor, or a rule that a hit must score on the vector leg, inside semantic-projection.ts, plus a unit case. The live-proof script also needs an assertion that the pizza line is gone, run on the deployed api container.
  - evidence: src/features/person-model/services/semantic-projection.ts:88-119: relatedRecall filters hits with no score threshold (RELATED_FETCH=12, RELATED_CAP=6 at :21-22). src/features/rag/services/pgvector-rag-engine.ts:159 computes `1 - (embedding <=> $2)`, but :204 returns reciprocalRankFusion output; hybrid-fusion.ts:13 sets RRF_K=60. Fixture scripts/person-model-live-proof.js:41-46 seeds 'Can we order pizza tonight'. The asserts at :68-71 never check that it is absent. The script runs by docker exec in oshal-local-api (header :9-10).

- **Complete and cancel nightly backup checks safely**  `M · docker · core`  _(Connectors, channels, and external systems)_
  - The trunk's backup diagnostic still has the exact defect that was observed. psql runs with ON_ERROR_STOP=0 inside `sh -c` without pipefail, so a cancelled or failed pg_dump still exits 0. Only the `agents` row count is compared, so a partial restore can pass. There is no statement/lock timeout and no check of the deploy lock. The fix is hardening this script (strict exit codes, full-restore verification, bounded run and cleanup, deploy-lock coordination) plus regressions registered in the AI Test Lab. Those regressions must hit a real Postgres to prove the lock behaviour. The scheduled-launcher leg overlaps BACKLOG 'Nightly tasks still launched from the ADR-115 archive'.
  - evidence: scripts/evidence/prove-local-selfhost-live.ts:100 (`pg_dump … | psql -v ON_ERROR_STOP=0 …` in sh -c, no pipefail). :33 COUNT_TABLE='agents' is the only equality check (:102-106). :94-110 has no timeout and no lock check. scripts/oshal-deploy.sh:58,80: the deploy lock at $STATE_DIR/lock is never consulted by the diagnostic. scripts/run-evidence-nightly-hidden.vbs:9 hardcodes C:\Projects\open-shal-swarm-harness-agent-llm (the archive checkout). docs/BACKLOG.md:349-351 tracks the launcher repoint. A grep of src/app/routes/test-lab-*.ts for backup|restore finds no Lab scenario.

- **Political-trades (STOCK Act) signal has never run on this box**  `M · docker · both`  _(Trading and market systems)_
  - The 'no world_* table' premise was probably checked against the wrong database. world_metrics lives in the separate TimescaleDB (oshal-tsdb/oshal_ts), world intelligence and the flow collectors are on by default, and a 2026-09-14 commit measured live world_metrics reads on that store. Whether congress_* rows actually exist there is still unconfirmed. The rest of the done-when is not met in code: the collector writes per-ticker counts timestamped now() and throws away each disclosure's ReportDate, and the trading watchlist has no feed-sourced population path, disclosure date, or guard against non-feed congressional entries.   - Neither the arming acknowledgement nor the 'enabled never widens' guard exists. Clause 3 of the done-when is now contradicted by ADR-159, so it has to be reworded before this is built. One thing bears on the 'delivery failure remains visible' criterion: the hook's .catch only handles thrown errors, and the transport returns delivered:false rather than throwing. A failed send therefore shows up only as the transport's warn log, not on the run ledger, and there is no retry.

B3 (face-to-bearing tracking), B5 (servo load, inertia and stall in the rehearsal) and B6 (jaw driven by an audio envelope, triggered by a Pumpkin browser hand-off) are code an agent can write in the store package now, together about L. Agents can build B3, B5 and B6 in the store package meanwhile.

- **The bot database role can read almost nothing it was meant to (2026-09-14)**  _(Animatronics and the maker labs)_
  - The entry's premise and approach conflict with the governed bot contract on main. oshal_bot has an exact allowlist, re-converged and verified on every api boot by the provisioner. The '3 of 409' figure matches exactly the three table-level SELECT grants; agents and chat_tasks have column-level SELECT grants, which that count missed. The contract's defaults FOR ROLE oshal_app deliberately revoke from oshal_bot, and a wholesale migration grant is stripped on the next boot. The real live gap is the ADR-149 posture read, which still fails after every api restart. Its fix is draft PR #459, which also deletes this entry as 'built on a misreading'. That PR explicitly waits for the operator to approve amending the bot contract.
  - evidence: docs/governance/app-role-provisioning.sql (3ca043c1, 2026-09-11, before the 09-14 entry): full-table SELECT only on persona_layers L347, work_items L349 and tickets L375; column-level grants on agents L331-334 and chat_tasks L353-368; ALTER DEFAULT PRIVILEGES FOR ROLE oshal_app ... REVOKE ... FROM oshal_bot at L193-202. scripts/governance/provision-app-role.mjs L264-393 fails on any 'unexpected effective oshal_bot' privilege. The live strip: ~/.oshal-deploy/deploy-20260915-091435.log 'VERIFY FAIL bot-role-grant oshal_bot cannot SELECT public.oshal_authorization_applications ... stripped again 
  - **ask:** Approve or reject draft core PR #459 (fix/bot-ownership-helper). It adds a SECURITY DEFINER helper, oshal_application_execution_claims, to the governed oshal_bot contract (an ADR-076 amendment), removes migration 140, and deletes this backlog entry. If you approve, merge #459 and close this entry as superseded. Do not pursue this entry's own grant-wholesale and default-privilege approach: the provisioner reverts it on every boot.
